# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-20

This edition provides 84 complete documentation guides and 2 additional screened leads. 40 earlier screened entries received full guides. New and updated records carry their own dated evidence checks. Spatial-audio generation, film captions and camera-driven production illustrate different kinds of control. SAID maps detected sound events onto a panoramic view. Kdenlive’s subtitle workflow gives an editor text and timing inside an established non-linear editor. Other guides cover image-to-geometry handoffs, fabrication, lyric and notation drafts, and accessible media. Velo adds locally generated rehearsal textures to its live graph. ZigZag adds canvas-pinned feedback and version comparison for an AI-directed embroidery edit. Software licensing is reviewed independently of model terms, any paid service or required proprietary host. Documentation and published examples informed the quality checks. No discovered app was installed or run. The frozen plan covered 114 fields with 1306/1306 repository queries attempted. 1306 completed successfully, 0 were partial, and 0 failed. 253 were bounded. All 33 configured model tasks were attempted. 0 model tasks failed. The collection retained explicit warnings and limits. Metadata screening indexed all 2,779 saved repository pages, spanning 83,958 numeric identities. The complete retained backlog was consulted alongside today's metadata, including earlier pilot candidates. It currently has 117,114 raw records and 1,132 previously screened pending guides. A metadata signal does not establish software eligibility. Unreviewed and excluded candidates remain in private research records. Live primary research captured 460 usable responses from 600 distinct URL attempts, including non-GitHub primary sources in 114 of 114 fields. 8 captures were size-bounded. The ecosystem notes record each configured source group. The notes identify limits such as access and rate restrictions, failed fetches and bounded discovery pages. The counts represent the documented searches and available pages. Evidence label counts were 0 Recommended, 1 Ready to try, 80 Quality unverified, 5 Experimental. SAID meets the Ready to try criteria for the stated scope. The public Hugging Face Space at the checked revision, with the bundled examples and the released SAID PaSST checkpoint for noncommercial research. Credential-pattern material in unsealed source captures was redacted before sealing. The original and redacted content hashes and provenance stay in the local evidence record. 31 model tasks reached the configured maximum of 50 returned rows each. The model listings are bounded discovery leads. Model-task responses with no rows: text2text-generation, zero-shot-audio-classification. The separate watchlist and evidence enrichment recorded 63 individual watchlist failures and 4 evidence failures. Source failures are retained as gaps.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Search and review counts

114 creative fields; 1306/1306 repository queries attempted; 33/33 model-task queries attempted. 84758 distinct source candidates and 1432 model leads. 84 detailed profiles and 2 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 253 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## PhysWave

Spatial audio and sound dramaturgy · AI Foley and sound effects · WebXR, VR & AR · Audio, music & voice

This is the first complete guide in the library.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Sketching a moving cue for a sound installation, VR scene or spatial Foley study.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-20. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code. Models, services and assets have separate terms. [Source](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The developers publish examples of stationary cues, an arc of typing and an approaching or receding sound. The documented output is about ten seconds of 16 kHz first-order Ambisonics. [Source](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://lingfengyao.github.io/PhysWave/) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29549) [Source](https://huggingface.co/10wind/PhysWave) [Source](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/stable_audio_tools/LICENSE)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use a Python 3.10 environment with a CUDA GPU and install the released package from source. Fetch the named diffusion and autoencoder checkpoints. T5-base downloads on first use. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://lingfengyao.github.io/PhysWave/) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29549) [Source](https://huggingface.co/10wind/PhysWave) [Source](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/stable_audio_tools/LICENSE)
- **Maintenance and support · documented:** The maintainer answered a licensing question on October 27 and added explicit source and model terms. Other platform support and independent maintenance checks remain open. [Source](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/issues/1)
- **Independent use · unknown:** A third party discussed prospective integration. The current issue does not show a completed independent integration or its result. [Source](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/issues/1)
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Apache-2.0 covers the project code and MIT covers the named Stable Audio Tools subset. Released checkpoints and the dataset carry CC BY-NC 4.0 restrictions. Direct waypoint input uses local inference. The optional natural-language parser calls a separate Gemini or OpenRouter service. [Source](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://lingfengyao.github.io/PhysWave/) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29549) [Source](https://huggingface.co/10wind/PhysWave) [Source](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/stable_audio_tools/LICENSE)

This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. One source and a fixed short duration are the documented mode. A demo rendering leaves direction accuracy, listening quality and fit to a particular installation unverified.

### How it uses AI

A physics-guided diffusion model generates a sound event along an authored trajectory and renders a first-order Ambisonics WAV. The user can supply the trajectory as waypoints. [Source](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://lingfengyao.github.io/PhysWave/) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29549) [Source](https://huggingface.co/10wind/PhysWave) [Source](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/stable_audio_tools/LICENSE)

### Introduction

PhysWave lets a sound designer describe one short sound and trace its movement around a listener. For a study, the provided waypoint files give direct control over movement without a hosted parser. [Source 1](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://lingfengyao.github.io/PhysWave/) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29549) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/10wind/PhysWave) [Source 5](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/stable_audio_tools/LICENSE)

### What it is good for

Sketching a moving cue for a sound installation, VR scene or spatial Foley study. [Source 1](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://lingfengyao.github.io/PhysWave/) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29549) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/10wind/PhysWave) [Source 5](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/stable_audio_tools/LICENSE)

### Demo & examples

The developers publish examples of stationary cues, an arc of typing and an approaching or receding sound. The documented output is about ten seconds of 16 kHz first-order Ambisonics. [Source 1](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://lingfengyao.github.io/PhysWave/) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29549) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/10wind/PhysWave) [Source 5](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/stable_audio_tools/LICENSE)

### Install

Use a Python 3.10 environment with a CUDA GPU and install the released package from source. Fetch the named diffusion and autoencoder checkpoints. T5-base downloads on first use. [Source 1](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://lingfengyao.github.io/PhysWave/) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29549) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/10wind/PhysWave) [Source 5](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/stable_audio_tools/LICENSE)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use a Python 3.10 environment with a CUDA GPU and install the released package from source. Fetch the named diffusion and autoencoder checkpoints. T5-base downloads on first use.

```sh
python -m physwave --condition examples/keyboard_arc.json --output outputs/keyboard.wav
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://lingfengyao.github.io/PhysWave/) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29549) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/10wind/PhysWave) [Source 5](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/stable_audio_tools/LICENSE)

1. Start from a supplied waypoint file and inspect its caption, times and angles.
2. Change one movement parameter. Preserve the emitted JSON beside the WAV so another creator can trace the cue.
3. Decode to the actual headphone or loudspeaker layout and check front/back direction and the apparent path.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://lingfengyao.github.io/PhysWave/) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29549) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/10wind/PhysWave) [Source 5](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/stable_audio_tools/LICENSE)

- **Hardware:** A CUDA GPU is required by the documented setup. Minimum VRAM, memory and storage remain undetermined.
- **Software:** Python 3.10, CUDA-compatible runtime, both checkpoints and T5-base. A separate Ambisonics decoder is required for ordinary headphone monitoring.
- **Platforms:** The maintainer documents a CUDA workflow. Equivalent native macOS or CPU-only inference remains undocumented in the reviewed setup.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://lingfengyao.github.io/PhysWave/) [Source 4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29549) [Source 5](https://huggingface.co/10wind/PhysWave) [Source 6](https://github.com/lingfengyao/PhysWave/blob/main/stable_audio_tools/LICENSE)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 covers the project code and MIT covers the named Stable Audio Tools subset. Released checkpoints and the dataset carry CC BY-NC 4.0 restrictions.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 covers the project code and MIT covers the named Stable Audio Tools subset. Released checkpoints and the dataset carry CC BY-NC 4.0 restrictions.
- **Cost:** Direct waypoint input uses local inference. The optional natural-language parser calls a separate Gemini or OpenRouter service.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. ### Limitations

One source and a fixed short duration are the documented mode. A demo rendering leaves direction accuracy, listening quality and fit to a particular installation unverified. ## SAID

Spatial audio and sound dramaturgy · Physical, robotic & kinetic installations · AI assisted sound data art and corpus instruments · Audio, music & voice

This is the first complete guide in the library.

### Quality assessment: Ready to try

The exact public deployment, install configuration, maintainer documentation and an independent implementation are supported by current evidence. Accuracy and suitability for a new installation remain unverified.

Scope: The public Hugging Face Space at the checked revision, with the bundled examples and the released SAID PaSST checkpoint for noncommercial research.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-20. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code. Models, services and assets have separate terms. [Source](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** Maintainers publish narrow reference comparisons. We did not verify whether timing, source separation and location match a new installation or recording. [Source](https://huggingface.co/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/raw/6d01a3b19b92668686924e46c44aabec3d953295/README.md) [Source](https://dcase.community/workshop2026/proceedings#headingwang2026)
- **Reproducible setup · verified:** The independent implementer published the named Gradio configuration, exact dependencies and vendored inference source. Its public runtime reported Running on the same commit and the scoped checkpoint responded to a HEAD request. [Source](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/issues/1) [Source](https://huggingface.co/api/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/runtime) [Source](https://huggingface.co/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/raw/6d01a3b19b92668686924e46c44aabec3d953295/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/raw/6d01a3b19b92668686924e46c44aabec3d953295/app.py) [Source](https://huggingface.co/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/raw/6d01a3b19b92668686924e46c44aabec3d953295/requirements.txt) [Source](https://huggingface.co/api/models/IN03X/SAID)
- **Maintenance and support · verified:** The upstream maintainer responded to the independent integration and documents the current model extension. The exact supported PaSST demonstration was serving successfully at the time of source review. Ongoing external availability can vary. [Source](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/issues/1) [Source](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/docs/audio2sph_l.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/api/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/runtime)
- **Independent use · verified:** A Hugging Face open-source team member describes independently building and deploying the public demo. The pinned application imports SAID, retrieves the upstream PaSST checkpoint and serves the documented packaged examples. [Source](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/issues/1) [Source](https://huggingface.co/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/raw/6d01a3b19b92668686924e46c44aabec3d953295/app.py) [Source](https://huggingface.co/api/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/runtime)
- **Models, services and costs · verified:** The reviewed Gradio deployment uses the documented ZeroGPU allocation and exact dependency set. The scoped checkpoint is available under noncommercial research terms. The bundled test recordings have separately reviewed MIT notices. [Source](https://huggingface.co/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/raw/6d01a3b19b92668686924e46c44aabec3d953295/requirements.txt) [Source](https://huggingface.co/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/raw/6d01a3b19b92668686924e46c44aabec3d953295/app.py) [Source](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/LICENSES/SAID-Model-Weights-NonCommercial-1.0.txt) [Source](https://huggingface.co/api/models/IN03X/SAID) [Source](https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/spaces-zerogpu) [Source](https://huggingface.co/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/raw/6d01a3b19b92668686924e46c44aabec3d953295/STAIRS26-MIT.txt) [Source](https://huggingface.co/spaces/hugging-apps/said-semantic-acoustic-imaging/raw/6d01a3b19b92668686924e46c44aabec3d953295/STARSS23-MIT.txt)

Source review only. The deployment API reported Running on the archived commit. We did not run inference. Use the Space’s short bundled recordings for the scoped trial. The fit of its maps to a new microphone arrangement is unresolved. The checkpoints carry a noncommercial research license. External hosting availability and quota apply.

### How it uses AI

The project’s learned detector maps active sound events to labeled panoramic regions from spatial microphone recordings. Its CLI emits structured directional observations that can drive an authored visualization. [Source](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://dcase.community/workshop2026/proceedings#headingwang2026) [Source](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/LICENSES/SAID-Model-Weights-NonCommercial-1.0.txt) [Source](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/docs/inference.md)

### Introduction

SAID places sound events on a panoramic map. The event and direction data can help an artist connect a sound field to a display or organize a multichannel recording. [Source 1](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://dcase.community/workshop2026/proceedings#headingwang2026) [Source 3](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/LICENSES/SAID-Model-Weights-NonCommercial-1.0.txt) [Source 5](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/docs/inference.md)

### What it is good for

Prototyping an acoustic map for an installation or annotating events in a consented multichannel recording. [Source 1](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://dcase.community/workshop2026/proceedings#headingwang2026) [Source 3](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/LICENSES/SAID-Model-Weights-NonCommercial-1.0.txt) [Source 5](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/docs/inference.md)

### Demo & examples

The maintainer provides four synchronized comparisons with published reference and predicted acoustic maps. The video pairs and benchmark are narrowly scoped to the recorded datasets and model variant. [Source 1](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://dcase.community/workshop2026/proceedings#headingwang2026) [Source 3](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/LICENSES/SAID-Model-Weights-NonCommercial-1.0.txt) [Source 5](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/docs/inference.md)

### Install

Create an environment with Python 3.10–3.12, install the package from the source checkout and make FFmpeg available for visuals. The CLI retrieves the selected checkpoint on first run. [Source 1](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://dcase.community/workshop2026/proceedings#headingwang2026) [Source 3](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/LICENSES/SAID-Model-Weights-NonCommercial-1.0.txt) [Source 5](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/docs/inference.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Create an environment with Python 3.10–3.12, install the package from the source checkout and make FFmpeg available for visuals. The CLI retrieves the selected checkpoint on first run.

```sh
said demo --model said_passt
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://dcase.community/workshop2026/proceedings#headingwang2026) [Source 3](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/LICENSES/SAID-Model-Weights-NonCommercial-1.0.txt) [Source 5](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/docs/inference.md)

1. For the Ready to try scope, open the documented public demo and inspect a bundled recording first. Local or commercial operation requires its own evidence review.
2. Inspect the packaged reference scenes and decide how labeled regions should enter the visual work.
3. Validate microphone capsule order against the documented Eigenmike input before analyzing a new recording.
4. Keep the DCASE JSON with the recording, channel map and model identification.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://dcase.community/workshop2026/proceedings#headingwang2026) [Source 3](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/LICENSES/SAID-Model-Weights-NonCommercial-1.0.txt) [Source 5](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/docs/inference.md)

- **Hardware:** Input is specified for an Eigenmike signal or four selected capsules in the documented order. Minimum RAM, VRAM and disk space remain undocumented.
- **Software:** Python 3.10–3.12, FFmpeg for demos, and the appropriate model artifact. DCASE JSON is the documented default inference output.
- **Platforms:** The reviewed source describes a Python setup. Verified packaged builds and support on each operating system remain pending.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://dcase.community/workshop2026/proceedings#headingwang2026) [Source 4](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/LICENSES/SAID-Model-Weights-NonCommercial-1.0.txt) [Source 6](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/docs/inference.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** The project’s original software is MIT. Specified third-party components have their own grants. Published model checkpoints carry a noncommercial research license.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. The project’s original software is MIT. Specified third-party components have their own grants. Published model checkpoints carry a noncommercial research license.
- **Cost:** The software supports local processing. Specialized capture hardware, model access and dataset permission vary with the intended workflow.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://dcase.community/workshop2026/proceedings#headingwang2026) [Source 3](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/LICENSES/SAID-Model-Weights-NonCommercial-1.0.txt) [Source 5](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/docs/inference.md)

### Limitations

The default example uses the paper’s PaSST model. The newer SAID-L route includes training code and currently lacks a published pretrained checkpoint according to the maintainer. [Source 1](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://dcase.community/workshop2026/proceedings#headingwang2026) [Source 3](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/LICENSES/SAID-Model-Weights-NonCommercial-1.0.txt) [Source 5](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/docs/inference.md)

### Get the tool

- [Documentation](https://github.com/IN03X/SAID/blob/main/README.md)

## Ukuvula

AI museum experiences and participatory exhibition media · Archives, media restoration & collections · Creative publishing & presentation · Accessible media & assistive creation

This is the first complete guide in the library.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Building an initial text index and citation trail for a complex, rights-cleared set of oral recordings.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-20. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code. Models, services and assets have separate terms. [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The developer documents its Nelson Mandela Foundation corpus example, with archival clip comparisons, sample transcripts, confidence information and visual analysis. [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/requirements.txt)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the supplied conda environment with Python 3.10–3.11. Configure the metadata and media inputs for a single collection. Keep the optional cloud stages disabled when they are outside the collection’s permissions. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/requirements.txt)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT covers project code. Licenses and access terms for speech models, diarization, cloud services and archival recordings are separate. The transcription route uses local compute. Optional Azure OpenAI analysis uses a separate account and service terms. [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/requirements.txt)

This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. Published collection examples leave accuracy on new accents, quiet speech, overlapping speakers and degraded recordings unresolved. A human editor still needs to proof names and attributable quotations.

### How it uses AI

WhisperX creates timestamped text from recordings and the pipeline aggregates transcripts and confidence information. Optional Azure OpenAI stages extract entities and summarize themes separately. [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### Introduction

Ukuvula is an archival transcription workflow with separate metadata, speech, aggregation and enrichment stages. A collection editor can keep generated text tied to the recording and review it before publication. [Source 1](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/microsoft/ukuvula/blob/main/requirements.txt)

Building an initial text index and citation trail for a complex, rights-cleared set of oral recordings. ## Kdenlive speech tools

Editing, captions & post-production · Video, animation & film · Accessible media & assistive creation · AI museum experiences and participatory exhibition media

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

- **Creative results · documented:** The 26.08 manual shows how recognized speech becomes subtitles in the editor. A third-party guide describes the workflow for 26.08.2. A current exact-version independent setup and representative caption comparison remain open. ### How it uses AI

The editor can turn selected timeline or clip audio into timed subtitles using its Whisper or Vosk engine, then lets the editor correct and export the caption track. [Source](https://github.com/KDE/kdenlive/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://invent.kde.org/multimedia/kdenlive/-/raw/master/COPYING) [Source](https://docs.kdenlive.org/en/effects_and_filters/speech_to_text.html) [Source](https://docs.kdenlive.org/en/getting_started/configure_kdenlive/configuration_plugins.html) [Source](https://docs.kdenlive.org/en/getting_started/installation.html) [Source](https://kdenlive.org/download/) [Source](https://kdenlive.org/news/releases/26.08.3/) [Source](https://news.updatestar.com/amp/post/kdenlive-auto-subtitles-let-whisper-do-the-typing)

### Introduction

Kdenlive keeps a speech-recognition draft in the same timeline where the editor has the source cut. For exhibition and documentary work, this makes it practical to check subtitle timing against the image before sharing an SRT. ### How it uses AI

The plugin suite runs local neural audio effects in Audacity. Its Whisper tool writes a label track from a selected recording. The separate Demucs-based effect can generate individual music stems. [Source](https://github.com/intel/openvino-plugins-ai-audacity/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/intel/openvino-plugins-ai-audacity/releases/tag/v3.7.1-R4.2) [Source](https://github.com/intel/openvino-plugins-ai-audacity/blob/main/doc/feature_doc/whisper_transcription/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/intel/openvino-plugins-ai-audacity/blob/main/doc/build_doc/linux/README.md) [Source](https://admin.kuleuven.be/icts/english/research/datamgmtpract/transcribe/transcription_audacity)

### Introduction

This extension brings model-based editing into an Audacity project. A label track, stem tracks and enhancement effects can each be reviewed independently from the source audio. ## AMBIT

Spatial audio and sound dramaturgy · AI Foley and sound effects · WebXR, VR & AR · Audio, music & voice

The source repository was created on 2026-10-23. This is its first complete guide in the library.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Making a short draft of a moving sound source or adjusting one property of a spatial recording for an installation.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-20. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code. Models, services and assets have separate terms. [Source](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The authors report evaluation of generated and edited spatial sounds. The code documents 44.1 kHz four-channel WAV outputs, saved scene plans and a roughly fifteen-second reference limit for editing. [Source](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34415) [Source](https://huggingface.co/WJM-George/AMBIT) [Source](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Prepare the documented Linux and CUDA environment with Python 3.10 or later and a GPU supporting bfloat16. Install from source and place the released model bundle in the named directory. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34415) [Source](https://huggingface.co/WJM-George/AMBIT) [Source](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT covers AMBIT source. The published bundle card declares MIT. Third-party components listed in the notices, referenced sound recordings and any data used to train or edit remain separate. Local GPU processing plus model storage. The scoped CLI calls the downloaded model. [Source](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34415) [Source](https://huggingface.co/WJM-George/AMBIT) [Source](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. The current scope is short clips and first-order Ambisonics. Exact VRAM, end-to-end runtime and preservation of unrelated sounds need measurement on the intended workflow.

### How it uses AI

A generative model converts a text request into a structured sound scene plan and then renders a new or edited first-order Ambisonics clip. The editing path conditions on a reference sound field. [Source](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34415) [Source](https://huggingface.co/WJM-George/AMBIT) [Source](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Introduction

AMBIT lets a sound designer save a spatial scene plan beside a WAV. The plan gives an editor something to inspect when a generated sound arrives from the wrong direction or a requested edit alters the room. [Source 1](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34415) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/WJM-George/AMBIT) [Source 4](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### What it is good for

Making a short draft of a moving sound source or adjusting one property of a spatial recording for an installation. [Source 1](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34415) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/WJM-George/AMBIT) [Source 4](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Demo & examples

The authors report evaluation of generated and edited spatial sounds. The code documents 44.1 kHz four-channel WAV outputs, saved scene plans and a roughly fifteen-second reference limit for editing. [Source 1](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34415) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/WJM-George/AMBIT) [Source 4](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

### Install

Prepare the documented Linux and CUDA environment with Python 3.10 or later and a GPU supporting bfloat16. Install from source and place the released model bundle in the named directory. [Source 1](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34415) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/WJM-George/AMBIT) [Source 4](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Prepare the documented Linux and CUDA environment with Python 3.10 or later and a GPU supporting bfloat16. Install from source and place the released model bundle in the named directory.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34415) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/WJM-George/AMBIT) [Source 4](https://github.com/WJM-George/AMBIT/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Generate a short, single-event spatial scene using the documented CLI.
2. Inspect the generated scene plan and retain it with the audio.
3. For an edit, supply a rights-cleared FOA reference in the expected channel order. Decode and compare the output in the target listening environment.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. ### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Scope: Converting a small oral-history collection into editable transcripts, timed excerpts and a reviewable project structure.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code. Models, services and assets have separate terms. [Source](https://github.com/Scholarly-Projects/ohmsi-kit/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The source describes batch and corrective paths plus a sample project structure. Transcript quality for the selected recording remains unknown. [Source](https://github.com/Scholarly-Projects/ohmsi-kit/blob/main/README.md)

Confirm the pinned Torch dependencies. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Scholarly-Projects/ohmsi-kit/blob/main/README.md)

This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. Speaker boundaries and word accuracy require editorial review, especially for overlap or conversation switching languages.

### How it uses AI

The toolkit applies Whisper speech recognition and SpeechBrain-based diarization to structure interviews by speaker and time, with a manual editorial stage before publishing. [Source](https://github.com/Scholarly-Projects/ohmsi-kit/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

This kit organizes transcription and correction into separate steps. An oral-history editor can begin with a batch pass and spend manual time on the recordings that need it. [Source 1](https://github.com/Scholarly-Projects/ohmsi-kit/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Converting a small oral-history collection into editable transcripts, timed excerpts and a reviewable project structure. [Source 1](https://github.com/Scholarly-Projects/ohmsi-kit/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The source describes batch and corrective paths plus a sample project structure. Transcript quality for the selected recording remains unknown. [Source 1](https://github.com/Scholarly-Projects/ohmsi-kit/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use the documented Python 3.11 environment and FFmpeg. Follow the operating-system-specific setup before running the batch stage. Confirm the pinned Torch dependencies. [Source 1](https://github.com/Scholarly-Projects/ohmsi-kit/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the documented Python 3.11 environment and FFmpeg. Follow the operating-system-specific setup before running the batch stage. Confirm the pinned Torch dependencies.

```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Scholarly-Projects/ohmsi-kit/blob/main/README.md)

1. Place an approved recording inside the documented collection structure.
2. Begin with the batch transcript route. Compare speaker boundaries and text against the audio.
3. Move the passages needing deeper review through the corrective stage and preserve the chosen edit.

This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. GPU and CPU routes have different throughput. Quality on noisy, multilingual or overlapping speech remains a workflow-specific evidence gap.

### How it uses AI

WhisperX produces word timings and pyannote separates speakers. An HTML review page can follow the transcript as a recording plays. [Source](https://github.com/vkorost/mp3-transcribe/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

mp3-transcribe produces subtitles, structured timestamps and a portable transcript viewer from one recording. The viewer makes it easier to trace a proposed caption back to the source audio. [Source 1](https://github.com/vkorost/mp3-transcribe/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Preparing an editable, time-indexed interview transcript for an exhibit or video cut. [Source 1](https://github.com/vkorost/mp3-transcribe/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The maintainer publishes screenshots of the output, an audio-synced viewer and export examples. Exact accuracy for another recording remains unknown. [Source 1](https://github.com/vkorost/mp3-transcribe/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use the documented Python, FFmpeg, PyTorch and WhisperX setup. Obtain approved access for the selected diarization models. Use the CLI’s setup check before a batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/vkorost/mp3-transcribe/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the documented Python, FFmpeg, PyTorch and WhisperX setup. Obtain approved access for the selected diarization models. Use the CLI’s setup check before a batch.

```sh
mp3transcribe --check
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/vkorost/mp3-transcribe/blob/main/README.md)

1. Start with one cleared recording in a test folder.
2. Review overlaps and timing in the HTML viewer. Correct speaker labels by listening to the original.
3. Export structured JSON, text or timed captions for the publication workflow.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/vkorost/mp3-transcribe/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The author reports an RTX 4060 with 8 GB VRAM as a test configuration and estimates roughly 8 GB of runtime and model disk usage on the CUDA route.
- **Software:** Python with WhisperX and PyTorch, FFmpeg, Whisper large-v3, wav2vec2 alignment and gated pyannote artifacts.
- **Platforms:** The developer describes Linux, Windows and macOS. The documented macOS route uses CPU. NVIDIA acceleration is optional for applicable hosts.

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/vkorost/mp3-transcribe/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/vkorost/mp3-transcribe/blob/main/README.md)

- **Weights:** MIT covers the application. Gated diarization models require their own terms and approved access. Input recordings and names are governed separately.

### How it uses AI

A BMF module routes video frames through Real-ESRGAN and back into an editable, reproducible decode–process–encode graph. The upstream framework supplies the conversion and media plumbing. ### Introduction

BMF lets an artist or developer put an AI enhancement step inside an explicit video graph. The model, frame conversion and export settings can each be inspected when a result changes. This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. Requires camera geometry and consistent capture. Textured export alone does not establish dimensional accuracy or printable mesh readiness.

### How it uses AI

The released PyTorch pipeline learns a deformable-anchor representation from multi-view images and estimated cameras to reconstruct an implicit surface and export a mesh. [Source](https://github.com/3D-FRONT-FUTURE/NeuDA/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://3d-front-future.github.io/neuda) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.02375) [Source](https://colmap.github.io/)

### Introduction

NeuDA reconstructs a surface from images taken around an object. It fits a technical capture workflow where an editor can evaluate the cameras and geometry before using the mesh. [Source 1](https://github.com/3D-FRONT-FUTURE/NeuDA/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3d-front-future.github.io/neuda) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.02375) [Source 4](https://colmap.github.io/)

### What it is good for

Reconstructing a reference object from a calibrated image set for a 3D study or collection record. [Source 1](https://github.com/3D-FRONT-FUTURE/NeuDA/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3d-front-future.github.io/neuda) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.02375) [Source 4](https://colmap.github.io/)

### Demo & examples

The authors publish surface comparisons and a sample dataset route. The repository includes a textured-mesh path and links to published example assets. [Source 1](https://github.com/3D-FRONT-FUTURE/NeuDA/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3d-front-future.github.io/neuda) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.02375) [Source 4](https://colmap.github.io/)

### Install

Install the repository requirements in an isolated environment for the surface route. Custom data require COLMAP and the documented camera conversion. Optional adversarial texture optimization uses Blender 3.4 plus compiled extensions. [Source 1](https://github.com/3D-FRONT-FUTURE/NeuDA/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3d-front-future.github.io/neuda) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.02375) [Source 4](https://colmap.github.io/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the repository requirements in an isolated environment for the surface route. Custom data require COLMAP and the documented camera conversion. Optional adversarial texture optimization uses Blender 3.4 plus compiled extensions.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/3D-FRONT-FUTURE/NeuDA/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3d-front-future.github.io/neuda) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.02375) [Source 4](https://colmap.github.io/)

1. Use the public scene example to check layout of images, masks and camera data.
2. Verify a custom capture’s camera poses before starting reconstruction.
3. Review the mesh against reference views and inspect topology before downstream use.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/3D-FRONT-FUTURE/NeuDA/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3d-front-future.github.io/neuda) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.02375) [Source 4](https://colmap.github.io/)

- **Hardware:** The documented training command accepts a GPU selection. No minimum RAM, VRAM or storage threshold was established.
- **Software:** PyTorch dependencies, COLMAP for custom capture, and separate optional Blender 3.4 and CUDA/C++ texture dependencies.
- **Platforms:** The source examples show a GPU/training workflow and Linux-specific build commands for the optional texture route. Full Windows and macOS parity remains undocumented.

### Introduction

Swaralipi is a source-documented experiment in learning with a signing avatar. It lets a creator inspect the gloss and avatar movement for a small vocabulary before considering wider use. [Source 1](https://github.com/antony-jacob817/Swaralipi/blob/main/README.md)

3. Preserve the approved script and note the model and sign vocabulary used.

The pipeline separates stems, estimates rhythmic and note data using learned models and assigns guitar or bass notes to strings and frets for editable tablature. tabgen gives a guitarist editable notes and a suggested fingering from a recorded excerpt. Its separation between pitch and fretting makes revisions easier to trace. This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. Single-image reconstruction estimates occluded sides and scale. Export does not establish measured or printable geometry.

### How it uses AI

The extension runs a learned single-image reconstruction workflow in Forge Neo, with project-specific settings for generating a Gaussian splat and carrying it to a downstream tool. [Source](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://huggingface.co/speridlabs/idol) [Source](https://huggingface.co/Comfy-Org/Idol)

### Introduction

Idol for Forge Neo turns an approved object photograph into an inspectable splat. The actual model crop and saved settings help a 3D editor diagnose artifacts before export. [Source 1](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/speridlabs/idol) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/Comfy-Org/Idol)

### What it is good for

Creating a spatial reference from an approved object image for review in a staging or 3D application. [Source 1](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/speridlabs/idol) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/Comfy-Org/Idol)

### Demo & examples

The developer shows inputs and rotating object previews and describes export options and saved settings. Sides unseen by the camera are model estimates. [Source 1](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/speridlabs/idol) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/Comfy-Org/Idol)

### Install

Use the documented extension route in a compatible Forge Neo host and choose the specific checkpoint, image encoder, VAE and decoder listed for the workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/speridlabs/idol) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/Comfy-Org/Idol)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the documented extension route in a compatible Forge Neo host and choose the specific checkpoint, image encoder, VAE and decoder listed for the workflow.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/speridlabs/idol) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/Comfy-Org/Idol)

1. Prepare one cleared image with a visible object outline and useful angle.
2. Review the actual cropped model input and generated turntable.
3. Export a draft and check hidden surfaces, geometry and reuse terms in the receiving tool.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/eduardoabreu81/idol-forge-neo/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/speridlabs/idol) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/Comfy-Org/Idol)

- **Hardware:** The docs list specific component downloads. No independently verified minimum VRAM or whole-runtime storage requirement was established.
- **Software:** Forge Neo, named Idol checkpoint, DINO image encoder, FLUX.2 VAE and the specified decoder. Alternative components have separate loading instructions.
- **Platforms:** The extension is documented inside Forge Neo. Accelerator and support by operating system depend on the host and selected model.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. - **Commercial:** Software use follows AGPL-3.0. AGPLv3 covers project source. Adapted components are identified in NOTICE. Each weight and input image has separate terms.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://pypi.org/project/segan-gen/) [Source](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/tree/main/examples/godot) [Source](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/classes/class_audiostreamwav.html)

This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. Visual sound diagnostics help find clipping and loop discontinuities. Listening is still needed to judge timbre, mix balance and fit with a scene.

### How it uses AI

A compatible AI agent writes and revises an editable game-audio notation through an implemented MCP interface. SEGAN checks the notation, renders inspectable listen sheets and synthesizes output deterministically. [Source](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://pypi.org/project/segan-gen/) [Source](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/tree/main/examples/godot) [Source](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/classes/class_audiostreamwav.html)

### Introduction

SEGAN gives an AI-assisted game audio workflow editable source, deterministic renders and timing diagnostics. The game can load the produced audio without the agent that helped author it. [Source 1](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/project/segan-gen/) [Source 3](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/tree/main/examples/godot) [Source 4](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/classes/class_audiostreamwav.html)

### What it is good for

Building repeatable sound cues and music loops that a game developer can revise in text and import into Godot. [Source 1](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/project/segan-gen/) [Source 3](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/tree/main/examples/godot) [Source 4](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/classes/class_audiostreamwav.html)

### Demo & examples

The maintainer ships an example Godot project and documents WAV, loop-marker, manifest and resource output. Listen sheets show timing and spectrum for reviewing a generated revision. [Source 1](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/project/segan-gen/) [Source 3](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/tree/main/examples/godot) [Source 4](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/classes/class_audiostreamwav.html)

### Install

Use Python 3.11 or later with the documented segan-gen package. Configure the SEGAN MCP server in a compatible agent and point it at a dedicated audio project. The build also works independently from a manually edited score. [Source 1](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/project/segan-gen/) [Source 3](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/tree/main/examples/godot) [Source 4](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/classes/class_audiostreamwav.html)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Python 3.11 or later with the documented segan-gen package. Configure the SEGAN MCP server in a compatible agent and point it at a dedicated audio project. The build also works independently from a manually edited score.

```sh
python -m pip install segan-gen
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/project/segan-gen/) [Source 3](https://github.com/lukasgolino/segan/tree/main/examples/godot) [Source 4](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/classes/class_audiostreamwav.html)

1. Start from the documented example and ask the agent to make one short cue.
2. Lint the change and compare the waveform and loop seam with the previous build.
3. Listen at the intended game level and import the checked build into the engine.

## Velo

Performance, projection & stage media · Physical, robotic & kinetic installations · Camera-reactive projection installations · Audio, music & voice · Images & design

Velo v0.9.8-beta adds the local AI Image node and AI-panel integration. The preceding guide covered v0.9.7-beta.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Preparing a generated texture or visual cue for a VJ scene while retaining manual timing and an earlier playable scene.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-20. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the entire GPLv3 grant and the README’s GPL-3.0-or-later election for the desktop app. The complete MIT and Apache-2.0 library options were also reviewed. [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/LICENSE-GPL) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/LICENSE-APACHE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The release identifies the merged AI Image change. The implementing contributor documents end-to-end local rendering with stable-diffusion.cpp and timing measurements for specific models and settings. Immediate frame-time generation for a live cue was not established. [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/releases/tag/v0.9.8-beta) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/pull/4) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/docs/local-ai-plan.md) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/LICENSE-GPL) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use a matching release for the chosen platform or follow the documented Rust build. Configure a supported local image-serving endpoint and reviewed model separately. Generate images in rehearsal and verify the stored result before using the scene on stage. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/releases/tag/v0.9.8-beta) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/pull/4) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/docs/local-ai-plan.md) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/LICENSE-GPL) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)

Continued support across settings and platforms remains open. [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/pull/4) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/releases/tag/v0.9.8-beta)
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The shipped desktop app is GPL-3.0-or-later. Library components offer MIT or Apache-2.0. The external server, image model, recorded media and bundled drum weights have separate terms. The documented local route needs local compute. Separate image-model artifacts and any optional hosted agent or endpoint have independent terms. This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. Published timing and memory are contributor results for one setup. Preflight the cache, frame rate, external output and a fallback before a time-critical installation.

### How it uses AI

Velo v0.9.8-beta adds an AI Image node to its live graphics graph. The node requests locally generated images and loads the results for staging. The AI panel can place the node, and the project keeps the earlier live neural drum and melody analysis route. [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/releases/tag/v0.9.8-beta) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/pull/4) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/docs/local-ai-plan.md) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/LICENSE-GPL) [Source](https://github.com/g1lg1l/velo/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)

### Introduction

Velo’s latest beta makes locally generated still images available inside a live visual graph. That makes it possible to prepare a texture in rehearsal and carry it into a cue with the project. This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. PDF reading order, hyphenation and model pronunciation can be wrong. Scanned-PDF OCR is listed as planned. Use the original to verify the final accessible text.

### How it uses AI

A browser worker uses Kokoro text-to-speech to read text from a selected EPUB, text document or text-based PDF. WebGPU and WebAssembly are distinct execution paths, with built-in speech as an alternate. [Source](https://github.com/Blop77/hark/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://blop77.github.io/hark/) [Source](https://github.com/Blop77/hark/blob/main/src/tts/kokoro.worker.ts) [Source](https://huggingface.co/hexgrad/Kokoro-82M)

### Introduction

Hark makes a document audible from the browser and keeps text extraction separate from voice synthesis. It is useful for catching mismatches between printed and spoken reading order. [Source 1](https://github.com/Blop77/hark/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://blop77.github.io/hark/) [Source 3](https://github.com/Blop77/hark/blob/main/src/tts/kokoro.worker.ts) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/hexgrad/Kokoro-82M)

### What it is good for

Proof-listening a draft booklet, ebook or article and checking pronunciation and reading order. [Source 1](https://github.com/Blop77/hark/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://blop77.github.io/hark/) [Source 3](https://github.com/Blop77/hark/blob/main/src/tts/kokoro.worker.ts) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/hexgrad/Kokoro-82M)

### Demo & examples

The author publishes a browser build and source for the Kokoro worker, EPUB text selection and PDF cleanup. Word highlighting uses estimated timing. [Source 1](https://github.com/Blop77/hark/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://blop77.github.io/hark/) [Source 3](https://github.com/Blop77/hark/blob/main/src/tts/kokoro.worker.ts) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/hexgrad/Kokoro-82M)

### Install

Use a supported browser or serve the documented source workspace with its npm scripts. Allow the selected voice weights to finish downloading before depending on offline playback. [Source 1](https://github.com/Blop77/hark/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://blop77.github.io/hark/) [Source 3](https://github.com/Blop77/hark/blob/main/src/tts/kokoro.worker.ts) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/hexgrad/Kokoro-82M)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use a supported browser or serve the documented source workspace with its npm scripts. Allow the selected voice weights to finish downloading before depending on offline playback.

```sh
npm install
```


```sh
npm run dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Blop77/hark/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://blop77.github.io/hark/) [Source 3](https://github.com/Blop77/hark/blob/main/src/tts/kokoro.worker.ts) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/hexgrad/Kokoro-82M)

1. Start from a text-based PDF or short EPUB proof.
2. Compare reading order, headings, numbers and spoken names with the source.
3. Check browser offline behavior with the cached voice if the reading station needs it.

- **Platforms:** The maintainer describes faster WebGPU use in supported Chromium browsers and WebAssembly fallback in Firefox and Safari. Device and browser support still need checking for the chosen file.

### How it uses AI

The open provider and validation tools turn an authored score and a bounded interpretation prompt into a candidate performance map. The mockup can be rendered to WAV and compared to reference playback. ### Introduction

Rubato explores music encoded with both a fixed score and an allowed interpretive range. Its public harness makes a limited model-guided interpretation inspectable beside deterministic output. ### How it uses AI

RightSub has a scoped AI path that can stage a subtitle translation with local Ollama and return translated text to an SRT while preserving matching and direction processing. Its separate browser repair features use deterministic rules. ### Introduction

RightSub keeps bidirectional formatting repair distinct from model-assisted translation. That lets an editor compare one change at a time against the source subtitles. ### Introduction

OpenVideoForge gives an agent and animator a shared scene preview. The separate local Blender route can render a reviewed scene when its dependencies are in place. [Source 1](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

### What it is good for

Planning camera blocking and simple asset placement for a short animatic with an inspectable scene. [Source 1](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

### Demo & examples

The maintainer provides browser previews, examples and an MCP integration description. MP4 and full renderer output depend on separate local components. [Source 1](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

### Install

Use the project’s Node 18+ startup instructions for browser preview. Configure a supported agent interface separately. Provision compatible Blender and the optional small Python rendering service for the documented Blender route. [Source 1](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the project’s Node 18+ startup instructions for browser preview. Configure a supported agent interface separately. Provision compatible Blender and the optional small Python rendering service for the documented Blender route.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

1. Start with a short blocking scene and keep a copy before accepting agent edits.
2. Review camera timing, asset positions and framing in the browser.
3. Check one exported frame sequence using a matched local renderer before expecting a final video.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Node.js 18+, browser, and supported client for its MCP bridge. Blender 4.2 LTS and the described Python FastAPI/Uvicorn rendering service are needed for the named render workflow.
- **Platforms:** The project offers Windows launcher instructions and a Node/browser route. The browser preview does not establish platform parity for desktop rendering.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT covers project code. The third-party notices distinguish Three.js, external geometry data and fonts. The chosen models and incoming assets have their own terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers project code. The third-party notices distinguish Three.js, external geometry data and fonts. The chosen models and incoming assets have their own terms.
- **Cost:** The browser and Blender routes use local compute. Model providers depend on the external agent profile.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

### Limitations

Browser previsualization and Blender export have different dependencies. Keep draft timings and geometry open to correction before commit time. [Source 1](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/Lance-9527/OpenVideoForge/blob/main/README.md)

## RECCoon

Mobile, edge & on-device creation · Audio, music & voice · Editing, captions & post-production · Accessible media & assistive creation

This is the first complete guide in the library.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Recording a narration rehearsal or authorized interview in the field and bringing timed transcript material back to an edit.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-20. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code. Models, services and assets have separate terms. [Source](https://git.tomcooks.com/reccoon/plain/LICENSE?h=v1.1.2)
- **Creative results · documented:** The project publishes recorder and transcriber documentation and a v1.1.2 source tag. F-Droid describes the workflow and lists its build separately. The source and store versions need to be checked independently. - **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use a compatible arm64 Android device and the authorized F-Droid package or the documented source-build route. Download the separately listed model while network access is available, then try a short recording before going into the field. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. - **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** GPL-3.0-or-later covers software. The maintainer identifies CC BY 4.0 for Parakeet v3. The runtime and material recorded have their own terms. The scoped route runs on the device. It requires the initial model download and local recording storage. This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. A noisy room, multiple speakers and overlapping dialogue can affect the result. Check model download and the desired input before depending on a transcript in a remote location.

### How it uses AI

An Android field recorder uses a separately downloaded multilingual Parakeet v3 model through sherpa-onnx. It attaches editable text and SRT exports to a local recording and offers per-word seeking. RECCoon ties microphone checks, cue markers and a locally generated transcript to one file. Exporting the timed draft gives an editor a starting point while retaining the original audio. Mobile, edge & on-device creation · Editing, captions & post-production · Audio, music & voice · Accessible media & assistive creation

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Pulling a text draft from a short interview clip or field recording before sharing it with an editor.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-20. No local execution implied.

- **Creative results · documented:** The packaging source lists a current arm64 Android build. An independent user reported getting a result from a two-minute selection and having long jobs remain at 0% progress. The report does not establish quality or exact device parity. [Source](https://github.com/23rd/Scrib/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/org.scrib.transcriber/) [Source](https://github.com/23rd/Scrib/issues/48) [Source](https://github.com/23rd/Scrib/issues/49)

- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The repository contains the full GPLv3 text. F-Droid lists its package GPL-3.0-or-later. Exact speech models, runtime and contributed media have separate terms. The scoped route uses local device compute and needs initial model storage and download. This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. The firsthand report leaves long-clip processing, model and device comparisons unresolved. An optional remote endpoint has a different upload and privacy boundary.

### How it uses AI

Scrib uses a device-side speech model to transcribe a selected audio file. The scoped F-Droid build supports multiple compatible ggml models and offers a reusable Android transcription interface. Scrib puts an audio-to-text draft on the phone that already holds the recording. Choose the on-device build and check shorter clips first if the transcript must be ready quickly.
