## Anti-Vocale

Mobile, edge & on-device creation · Editing, captions & post-production · Accessible media & assistive creation · Audio, music & voice

This is the first complete guide in the library.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Drafting accessible captions or editorial transcripts from an approved recording before moving to a desktop editor.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-20. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code. Models, services and assets have separate terms. [Source](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The publisher provides device-aware model guidance and F-Droid documents video and subtitle export. Its benchmark descriptions are author results. A reported Parakeet model download-integrity issue shows why checking the exact installed package matters. [Source](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.antivocale.app/) [Source](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/issues/187)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the documented Android package and download a compatible on-device model for the chosen language. Start with an authorized short file and confirm the subtitle export format before transcribing a long recording. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.antivocale.app/) [Source](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/issues/187)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Apache-2.0 covers the app. Built-in or imported ASR models can have different terms. Voice, video and transcripts retain rights independent from the app. Device inference uses local compute and model storage. This guide scopes the local path. [Source](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.antivocale.app/) [Source](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/issues/187)

This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. Long recording support is limited by the chosen engine and hardware. Segmentation or live language choices can alter timing and speaker clues.

### How it uses AI

A native Android tool runs a selected speech model on a user-shared audio or video recording. Long clips can be segmented and returned as timed text, SRT or VTT. [Source](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.antivocale.app/) [Source](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/issues/187)

### Introduction

Anti-Vocale creates a portable timed subtitle draft from a file shared to an Android app. The model selector helps match speech language and device limits before a longer pass. [Source 1](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.antivocale.app/) [Source 3](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/issues/187)

### What it is good for

Drafting accessible captions or editorial transcripts from an approved recording before moving to a desktop editor. [Source 1](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.antivocale.app/) [Source 3](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/issues/187)

### Demo & examples

The publisher provides device-aware model guidance and F-Droid documents video and subtitle export. Its benchmark descriptions are author results. A reported Parakeet model download-integrity issue shows why checking the exact installed package matters. [Source 1](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.antivocale.app/) [Source 3](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/issues/187)

### Install

Use the documented Android package and download a compatible on-device model for the chosen language. Start with an authorized short file and confirm the subtitle export format before transcribing a long recording. [Source 1](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.antivocale.app/) [Source 3](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/issues/187)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the documented Android package and download a compatible on-device model for the chosen language. Start with an authorized short file and confirm the subtitle export format before transcribing a long recording.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.antivocale.app/) [Source 3](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/issues/187)

1. Use local files for the test and pick the intended ASR model.
2. Check caption gaps, line breaks, named entities and segment boundaries against the media.
3. Export SRT or VTT and make final timing corrections in the receiving editor.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.antivocale.app/) [Source 3](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/issues/187)

- **Hardware:** The app displays available model requirements and memory-tier fit. No single published memory minimum applies to all models.
- **Software:** Android device, a compatible distribution and a chosen model. Streaming, imported and built-in engines have distinct device requirements.
- **Platforms:** The project documents a native Android route. A desktop, iOS or browser workflow is outside this guide.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.antivocale.app/) [Source 4](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/issues/187)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 covers the app. Built-in or imported ASR models can have different terms. Voice, video and transcripts retain rights independent from the app.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 covers the app. Built-in or imported ASR models can have different terms. Voice, video and transcripts retain rights independent from the app.
- **Cost:** Device inference uses local compute and model storage. This guide scopes the local path.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.antivocale.app/) [Source 3](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/issues/187)

### Limitations

Long recording support is limited by the chosen engine and hardware. Segmentation or live language choices can alter timing and speaker clues. [Source 1](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.antivocale.app/) [Source 3](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/issues/187)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/RisorseArtificiali/anti-vocale/blob/main/README.md)

## VIVID

Video, animation & film · Editing, captions & post-production · AI agents for code-authored media production · AI assisted media quality and take selection

The source repository was created on 2026-11-16. This is its first complete guide in the library.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Exploring how an agent revises a short, cleared edit when a human can compare the rendered scene with its instructions and source.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-20. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code. Models, services and assets have separate terms. [Source](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The authors report benchmarks on 100 assembly, repair, sequencing and repurposing tasks and publish comparison cases. The gains apply to their benchmark setup and cited model versions. [Source](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20688) [Source](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/pyproject.toml)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use Python 3.11+, FFmpeg and ffprobe and the supported agent CLI. The docs distinguish direct media-inspection tools from the full managed Linux renderer. Provision the optional ASR dependency separately. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20688) [Source](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/pyproject.toml)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT covers VIVID software. Hosted model access, chosen agent products, benchmark material and ASR artifacts have separate terms. Direct tool runs use local compute. The managed route uses a configured OpenRouter provider by default, with model and request charges assessed separately. [Source](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20688) [Source](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. Differences in agent model, codecs and source material can alter the result. Frame samples omit information between images. Check the final edit and its format directly before using it downstream.

### How it uses AI

VIVID gives a coding agent model-assisted media inspection, frame and transcript references and a render-and-review loop for producing a video deliverable. Its tools keep intermediate evidence with the edited result. [Source](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20688) [Source](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

### Introduction

VIVID records what a coding agent inspected and revised during a video edit. That makes a draft and its rendered result easier to compare with the source brief. [Source 1](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20688) [Source 3](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

### What it is good for

Exploring how an agent revises a short, cleared edit when a human can compare the rendered scene with its instructions and source. [Source 1](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20688) [Source 3](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

### Demo & examples

The authors report benchmarks on 100 assembly, repair, sequencing and repurposing tasks and publish comparison cases. The gains apply to their benchmark setup and cited model versions. [Source 1](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20688) [Source 3](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

### Install

Use Python 3.11+, FFmpeg and ffprobe and the supported agent CLI. The docs distinguish direct media-inspection tools from the full managed Linux renderer. Provision the optional ASR dependency separately. [Source 1](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20688) [Source 3](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Python 3.11+, FFmpeg and ffprobe and the supported agent CLI. The docs distinguish direct media-inspection tools from the full managed Linux renderer. Provision the optional ASR dependency separately.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20688) [Source 3](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

1. Create an isolated edit workspace containing a short authorized clip and precise output instructions.
2. Inspect the agent’s chosen time ranges, frames and rendered result against the reference.
3. Retain the agent’s working records and correct any missing shots, captions or audio synchronization in the deliverable.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20688) [Source 3](https://github.com/yuwenhan07/VIVID/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.11+, FFmpeg and ffprobe, chosen agent CLI and Linux for managed rendering. Local recognizer installation and model downloads are separately documented.
- **Platforms:** Full managed rendering is documented on Linux. Other POSIX systems support the scoped inspection tools. Windows parity is unestablished.

- **Weights:** MIT covers VIVID software. Hosted model access, chosen agent products, benchmark material and ASR artifacts have separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers VIVID software. Hosted model access, chosen agent products, benchmark material and ASR artifacts have separate terms.

This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. A first-use download is required. The test of a model on a particular device and the effect of pauses or accented speech remain unverified.

### How it uses AI

Scribe streams speech through an on-device WhisperKit model and distinguishes draft hypotheses from confirmed text. A local system-speech option can read the result back. [Source](https://github.com/ehamiter/scribe/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/argmaxinc/argmax-oss-swift) [Source](https://huggingface.co/openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo)

### Introduction

Scribe makes the transient speech hypothesis visible while you dictate. It is useful for comparing the final text with a spoken line before it becomes part of a production draft. [Source 1](https://github.com/ehamiter/scribe/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/argmaxinc/argmax-oss-swift) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo)

### What it is good for

Capturing a spoken script draft or field notes and comparing recognized words before using the text in a production. [Source 1](https://github.com/ehamiter/scribe/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/argmaxinc/argmax-oss-swift) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo)

### Demo & examples

The maintainer documents a local source-build path for both platforms, a selectable model menu and different states during live transcription. Store distribution is intentionally on hold. [Source 1](https://github.com/ehamiter/scribe/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/argmaxinc/argmax-oss-swift) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo)

### Install

Open the documented Xcode workspace and use a local device or My Mac target. Allow the initial model download, then record a short passage to check both confirmed and draft text. [Source 1](https://github.com/ehamiter/scribe/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/argmaxinc/argmax-oss-swift) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Open the documented Xcode workspace and use a local device or My Mac target. Allow the initial model download, then record a short passage to check both confirmed and draft text.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ehamiter/scribe/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/argmaxinc/argmax-oss-swift) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo)

1. Dictate a short script with names, pauses and punctuation.
2. Wait for confirmed text and compare difficult phrases with the spoken wording.
3. Copy or review the corrected text in the intended publication workflow.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/ehamiter/scribe/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/argmaxinc/argmax-oss-swift) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo)

- **Hardware:** The developer describes a model range of approximately 75 MB to 1.5 GB. A common RAM or total disk minimum is not documented.
- **Software:** Xcode 26 or later for the source workspace, the WhisperKit Swift package and chosen model. Speech read-back uses available system voices.
- **Platforms:** The project lists macOS 14 and iOS/iPadOS 17 as minimum OS versions. The documented source path is a local build. Store and notarized packaging remain open.

### Introduction

Speech Note keeps speech recognition, read-back and translation as distinct editable steps. That helps an editor check a source transcript before creating another language version. ## ZigZag

Neural embroidery and stitch design · AI-assisted textile and computational craft · 3D printing & generative CAD · Vector graphics, illustration & textures

V0.1.6 adds canvas-pinned agent feedback, version comparisons, satins split into editable columns and stitch preflight since the profiled v0.1.2.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Revising embroidered lettering or a vector patch with an agent while keeping stitch order and proposed changes visible.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-20. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code. Models, services and assets have separate terms. [Source](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/LICENSE)

The project publishes platform assets and checksums. [Source](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/simpsoka/zigzag/releases/tag/v0.1.6)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the matching verified release for the documented desktop platform, then configure the built-in MCP entry point in a compatible model client. Start with a copy of a simple vector design that will fit the intended hoop. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. The selected agent account/model, fonts, artwork and machine formats have separate requirements or terms. The program can be used for local editing. Agent access and machine materials are separate. ## UniTEX

3D, reconstruction & assets · Neural procedural material authoring · Images & design · Neural denoising for real-time creative rendering

This is the first complete guide in the library.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Adding a draft texture to a licensed existing mesh and reviewing view consistency before carrying it into a game or previs scene.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-20. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code. Models, services and assets have separate terms. [Source](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The authors provide a CVPR paper, visual examples and code for a reference-image and mesh route. Results depend on the mesh, checkpoints and external rendering environment. [Source](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23253) [Source](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/requirements.txt) [Source](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/run.py)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Follow the documented Python 3.10 environment and CUDA 11.8 build path with compatible Torch, Kaolin and rendering dependencies. Review the listed checkpoint downloads and the documented SlangTorch compatibility note before use. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23253) [Source](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/requirements.txt) [Source](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/run.py)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Apache-2.0 covers the repository implementation. FLUX, model ports, LoRA and texturing checkpoints require separate terms review. Input models and references keep separate rights. Local CUDA computation and model storage. Exact provider access and model terms are separate. [Source](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23253) [Source](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/requirements.txt) [Source](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/run.py)

This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. Multi-view appearance coherence is not geometry accuracy. Seam quality, input variation and computational requirements remain specific to the mesh and exact setup.

### How it uses AI

The official texturing implementation uses learned multi-view generation and texture completion to create a surface appearance from a reference and input mesh. The workflow releases checkpoints separately from the code. [Source](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23253) [Source](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/requirements.txt) [Source](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/run.py)

### Introduction

UniTEX uses a reference image to draft an existing mesh’s appearance. Rotating the asset before reuse helps reveal completion artifacts at seams and unseen surfaces. [Source 1](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23253) [Source 3](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/requirements.txt) [Source 4](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/run.py)

### What it is good for

Adding a draft texture to a licensed existing mesh and reviewing view consistency before carrying it into a game or previs scene. [Source 1](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23253) [Source 3](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/requirements.txt) [Source 4](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/run.py)

### Demo & examples

The authors provide a CVPR paper, visual examples and code for a reference-image and mesh route. Results depend on the mesh, checkpoints and external rendering environment. [Source 1](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23253) [Source 3](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/requirements.txt) [Source 4](https://github.com/YixunLiang/UniTEX/blob/master/run.py)

### Install

Follow the documented Python 3.10 environment and CUDA 11.8 build path with compatible Torch, Kaolin and rendering dependencies. Review the listed checkpoint downloads and the documented SlangTorch compatibility note before use. This completes an earlier pending guide in the library. The current source review is dated 2026-11-20.

### Quality assessment: Experimental

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Prototyping an audio-described screening copy and inspecting which gaps can hold useful narration.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-20. No local execution implied.

Why Experimental: The current release is identified as a hackathon build, with demonstrated playback on a Vega Virtual Device and pending deployment or validation on retail hardware. https://github.com/CesarRivasP/Interstice/blob/main/README.md https://cesarrivasp.github.io/Interstice/e2e/ https://github.com/CesarRivasP/Interstice/releases/tag/v0.1.0-hackathon

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code. Models, services and assets have separate terms. [Source](https://github.com/CesarRivasP/Interstice/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The author ran a generated track on a Vega Virtual Device and evaluated 55 cues for the demo film. Two cues failed the author’s review. The source separates that generated path from a labelled playback fixture. - **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the documented Vega SDK and virtual-device setup for playback and the Node/FFmpeg pipeline for inspecting approved source media. Configure a supported vision provider and Polly separately if generating a fresh track. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/CesarRivasP/Interstice/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://cesarrivasp.github.io/Interstice/e2e/) [Source](https://github.com/CesarRivasP/Interstice/releases/tag/v0.1.0-hackathon)

Inspect each chosen deployment term separately. The developer describes using Gemini and Polly for its captured run. Hosted terms and billing vary by selected vision/audio providers. [Source](https://github.com/CesarRivasP/Interstice/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://cesarrivasp.github.io/Interstice/e2e/) [Source](https://github.com/CesarRivasP/Interstice/releases/tag/v0.1.0-hackathon)

This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. A timing budget can omit an important action, and an image model can describe the wrong object or movement. Narrator intelligibility and actual accessibility still need end-user review.

### How it uses AI

The project locates dialogue gaps, samples visual frames, asks a vision model for descriptions within a word budget and synthesizes a separate description track. A Vega app can duck the source audio around the cues. [Source](https://github.com/CesarRivasP/Interstice/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://cesarrivasp.github.io/Interstice/e2e/) [Source](https://github.com/CesarRivasP/Interstice/releases/tag/v0.1.0-hackathon)

### Introduction

Interstice calculates where description can fit without covering dialogue. The author’s cue-level review shows why a clear edit still needs someone to compare the narration with the film. [Source 1](https://github.com/CesarRivasP/Interstice/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://cesarrivasp.github.io/Interstice/e2e/) [Source 3](https://github.com/CesarRivasP/Interstice/releases/tag/v0.1.0-hackathon)

Prototyping an audio-described screening copy and inspecting which gaps can hold useful narration. This assessment is based on published sources. Hands-on verification remains pending. Generated animation can obscure type or drift from the brief. Review key frame boundaries, accessibility, export audio and scene timing before publication.

### How it uses AI

The desktop studio lets an AI agent author editable film source and inspect rendered frames. A person can point at an exact frame, request a revision and keep each edit as a version before exporting. [Source](https://github.com/motionhubdev/openmotionstudio/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://openmotion.studio/tour) [Source](https://github.com/motionhubdev/openmotionstudio/releases/tag/v0.1.3)

### Introduction

OpenMotionStudio makes a frame-specific revision visible in a versioned motion project. The scene source stays available for inspection after the edit is rendered. [Source 1](https://github.com/motionhubdev/openmotionstudio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://openmotion.studio/tour) [Source 3](https://github.com/motionhubdev/openmotionstudio/releases/tag/v0.1.3)

### How it uses AI

The model-facing skill directs hierarchy, grouping and a live-text SVG handoff. An included read-only Python checker reports structural issues for model-authored or edited vector work. ### Introduction

Golden Space directs an agent toward editable position, type and grouping choices. Its checker can flag structural SVG problems before a designer opens the master file. ### DiffPC · Apache-2.0

Investigating color compensation for a site-specific projection before committing to a physical setup.
A learned diffusion procedure compensates for photometric shifts when projector images interact with a physical surface. The research repository supplies training and test configurations.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. The source requires dataset paths and a trained model for evaluation. Minimum compute, checkpoint distribution terms, reproducible installation and results on an intended projector remain unresolved.

### See2Sound Backend · MIT

Exploring how model-drafted description can become a structured starting point for human narration and accessibility review.
The open backend implements vision and narrative stages to turn detected objects, timelines and scene information into a candidate audio-description script.
